# Prompts del historial de Curva

Modo: image_gen integrado. Se conserva el prompt de generación o la instrucción de edición recuperada para cada imagen.

## El miedo a la libertad · v1

Archivo: `originales/miedo-libertad-v1-f8bbdc5342.png`

Create ONE finished Spanish book-cover artwork for the Curva reading app. This is a new original cover, not an edit of a reference, not a mockup. The attached images are STYLE REFERENCES ONLY: use their premium flat editorial drawing, confident oversized typography, beautifully balanced large color fields, and very subtle cover finish. Do not copy their text, subjects, or exact composition.
OUTPUT: one portrait 2:3 cover, target 1024×1536 pixels. The artwork fills the canvas, front-on, with gently rounded corners and genuinely transparent pixels outside those corners. A delicate narrow pressed vertical crease close to the left edge, as in the references. No perspective, no visible side/spine, no table, no cast shadow outside the cover, no phone, no app UI, no surrounding margin. Crisp high-quality raster edges, primarily flat matte color, at most an almost imperceptible surface texture; avoid plastic/3D rendering, gradients within every shape, clip art, sketchiness, and dense decorative clutter.
Typography must feel deliberately designed as part of the illustration: clear Spanish lettering, careful line breaks, no distorted letters, comfortably inset from edges, readable at mobile thumbnail size. Render ONLY the exact title and short editorial line specified below, verbatim with correct accents. Do not add the author's name, year, publisher, logo, labels, captions, or any other text. Produce only this single finished cover.

BOOK: El miedo a la libertad, Erich Fromm. Content grounding: Fromm explores why people escape the anxiety of freedom through submission and conformity. The cover should feel intelligent, psychologically tense, and inviting to read.
ART DIRECTION: A striking warm butter-yellow background with deep ink-blue and one vermilion accent. In the lower half, an orderly crowd of about nine simplified people in identical navy clothes is moving toward the right, drawn as elegant graphic silhouettes with minimal facial features, not a mass of tiny stick figures. A single slightly larger coral-clothed person has stopped within the crowd and turns their head toward a tall OPEN blue doorway at the left; beyond the doorway is a clear warm-cream field. Their hand lightly holds the doorframe, a readable gesture of hesitation between an open possibility and the comfort of following others. The door, crowd, and one person should form one integrated editorial scene with strong, clean negative space. Simple believable anatomy, carefully designed silhouettes and overlapping planes. No birds, chains, padlocks, religious or political symbols.
TITLE: "El miedo a la libertad". Give the title monumental deep-navy heavy humanist sans-serif lettering across the upper part, with deliberate line breaks "El miedo" / "a la" / "libertad"; generous room for the last word and no overlap with the scene. Let the three lines create a compelling asymmetric rhythm rather than a boxed centered label.
SHORT EDITORIAL LINE: "Por qué elegimos obedecer". Set this in a substantially smaller but still confident navy sans-serif in a quiet area at the bottom right, on two lines. Keep the overall design bold, warm, and sparse enough to read instantly.

## Primavera silenciosa · v1

Archivo: `originales/primavera-silenciosa-v1-accd527bb2.png`

Create ONE finished Spanish book-cover artwork for the Curva reading app. This is a new original cover, not an edit of a reference, not a mockup. The attached images are STYLE REFERENCES ONLY: use their premium flat editorial drawing, confident oversized typography, beautifully balanced large color fields, and very subtle cover finish. Do not copy their text, subjects, or exact composition.
OUTPUT: one portrait 2:3 cover, target 1024×1536 pixels. The artwork fills the canvas, front-on, with gently rounded corners and genuinely transparent pixels outside those corners. A delicate narrow pressed vertical crease close to the left edge, as in the references. No perspective, no visible side/spine, no table, no cast shadow outside the cover, no phone, no app UI, no surrounding margin. Crisp high-quality raster edges, primarily flat matte color, at most an almost imperceptible surface texture; avoid plastic/3D rendering, gradients within every shape, clip art, sketchiness, and dense decorative clutter.
Typography must feel deliberately designed as part of the illustration: clear Spanish lettering, careful line breaks, no distorted letters, comfortably inset from edges, readable at mobile thumbnail size. Render ONLY the exact title and short editorial line specified below, verbatim with correct accents. Do not add the author's name, year, publisher, logo, labels, captions, or any other text. Produce only this single finished cover.

BOOK: Primavera silenciosa, Rachel Carson. Content grounding: the book investigates the environmental harm of indiscriminate pesticide use, including the disappearance of birds. The image is an ecological metaphor, not a technical diagram.
ART DIRECTION: A beautiful refined botanical editorial cover on warm ivory, with near-black forest green, saturated turquoise, a little ochre, and sparse vermilion. Across the upper two thirds, one large curved dark-green branch with broad graphic leaves carries a striking, recognizable small songbird in profile. Its solid ochre head and chest are intact, but the right-hand portion of its wing and tail quietly breaks into just a few separated leaf-shaped fragments that drift away; clean poetic disappearance, never gore or a shattered puzzle. The bird's beak is closed. Some foliage is lush and some twigs bare, conveying a spring becoming silent. A narrow understated band of geometric crop-field strips at the very bottom connects the nature scene to cultivated land without adding machinery or extra objects. Exquisite large shapes and visual rhythm, spacious, sophisticated, not a botanical engraving or watercolor.
TITLE: "Primavera silenciosa". Position the title prominently in the LOWER third, in two large lines "Primavera" / "silenciosa", using a heavy friendly editorial serif with simple thick strokes, dark forest green. It should dominate the cover as strongly as the reference titles, with no leaves obscuring letters.
SHORT EDITORIAL LINE: "El coste oculto de los pesticidas". Place this smaller near the upper left in three short legible lines, carefully separated from the bird and foliage. Strong typographic hierarchy, not a second huge headline. Finish as a beautiful polished cover that feels moving and contemporary.

## La guerra de los chips · v1

Archivo: `originales/guerra-chips-v1-2d965c8528.png`

Create ONE finished Spanish book-cover artwork for the Curva reading app. This is a new original cover, not an edit of a reference, not a mockup. The attached images are STYLE REFERENCES ONLY: use their premium flat editorial drawing, confident oversized typography, beautifully balanced large color fields, and very subtle cover finish. Do not copy their text, subjects, or exact composition.
OUTPUT: one portrait 2:3 cover, target 1024×1536 pixels. The artwork fills the canvas, front-on, with gently rounded corners and genuinely transparent pixels outside those corners. A delicate narrow pressed vertical crease close to the left edge, as in the references. No perspective, no visible side/spine, no table, no cast shadow outside the cover, no phone, no app UI, no surrounding margin. Crisp high-quality raster edges, primarily flat matte color, at most an almost imperceptible surface texture; avoid plastic/3D rendering, gradients within every shape, clip art, sketchiness, and dense decorative clutter.
Typography must feel deliberately designed as part of the illustration: clear Spanish lettering, careful line breaks, no distorted letters, comfortably inset from edges, readable at mobile thumbnail size. Render ONLY the exact title and short editorial line specified below, verbatim with correct accents. Do not add the author's name, year, publisher, logo, labels, captions, or any other text. Produce only this single finished cover.

BOOK: La guerra de los chips, Chris Miller. Content grounding: economic history and international competition over semiconductor design and manufacturing. The visual metaphor is the struggle to control a tiny but critical component.
ART DIRECTION: An exceptionally striking graphic cover with a saturated burnt-coral/orange field, a huge flat deep-cobalt microchip, warm cream, and a touch of ink navy. Two beautifully simplified human hands reach from opposite edges and grip opposite corners of the SAME oversized square semiconductor as if both want control of it. The main chip is frontal, slightly lower than center, large enough to be the central typographic surface. Its navy-blue square body and orderly short cream contact pins clearly read as a microchip, with a handful of clean circuit traces extending through the orange field toward the cover edges. Hands with natural proportions, clear fingers and convincing grip, one in warm ochre skin with navy sleeve, the other in warm cream skin with cobalt sleeve; no duplicate fingers, no fists striking anyone. Keep the composition flat, sophisticated and energetic. No maps, flags, weapons, chess pieces, faces, logos, glowing tech effects or tiny code.
TITLE: "La guerra de los chips". INTEGRATE the title directly INTO the large blue square of the microchip in oversized cream bold condensed sans-serif: "La guerra" / "de los" / "chips". The last word can be particularly large. It must look like intentional editorial lettering on the chip, with generous internal padding and no circuit lines crossing it, not a separate title strip above the illustration.
SHORT EDITORIAL LINE: "La lucha mundial por los semiconductores". Place it upper-left on the orange background, in three or four compact lines of smaller navy sans-serif; maintain distinct hierarchy and clear open space between it and the hands. Let the microchip, typography and opposing hands form one memorable visual idea.

The attached images are STYLE REFERENCES ONLY: use their premium flat editorial drawing, confident oversized typography, beautifully balanced large color fields, and very subtle cover finish. Do not copy their text, subjects, or exact composition.
OUTPUT: one portrait 2:3 cover, target 1024×1536 pixels. The artwork fills the canvas, front-on, with gently rounded corners and genuinely transparent pixels outside those corners. A delicate narrow pressed vertical crease close to the left edge, as in the references. No perspective, no visible side/spine, no table, no cast shadow outside the cover, no phone, no app UI, no surrounding margin. Crisp high-quality raster edges, primarily flat matte color, at most an almost imperceptible surface texture; avoid plastic/3D rendering, gradients within every shape, clip art, sketchiness, and dense decorative clutter.
Typography must feel deliberately designed as part of the illustration: clear Spanish lettering, careful line breaks, no distorted letters, comfortably inset from edges, readable at mobile thumbnail size. Render ONLY the exact title and short editorial line specified below, verbatim with correct accents. Do not add the author's name, year, publisher, logo, labels, captions, or any other text. Produce only this single finished cover.

BOOK: Mujeres que corren con los lobos, Clarissa Pinkola Estés. Content grounding: a Jungian reading of traditional tales about the archetype of the wild woman, instinct, and creative life. Use a mythic editorial scene, not fitness imagery or a literal wildlife documentary.
ART DIRECTION: A gorgeous dusky lavender and midnight-indigo cover with warm coral and cream accents. A confident adult woman with long dark hair, a simple flowing coral blouse and deep-indigo trousers strides dynamically toward the right alongside a large cream-colored wolf running freely. Both whole figures are clearly readable, the woman and wolf separate, never merged into a hybrid. Give the wolf elegant natural anatomy, a long unmistakable canine muzzle, four believable legs in a running gait, and one beautiful sweeping tail. The woman's hair and blouse echo the movement of the wolf without fusing with it. A few large stylized indigo forest silhouettes frame the scene at the edges; one ivory crescent sits in open lavender sky. Emotion: instinct, agency, folktale, quiet strength. Bold flat planes and purposeful silhouettes rather than childish storybook prettiness or fantasy realism.
TITLE: "Mujeres que corren con los lobos". Build a very strong cream typographic block in the upper part with comfortable margins: "Mujeres" / "que corren" / "con los lobos". Heavy warm rounded grotesk, large at every line, careful spacing; the title has enough breathing room and takes about the upper third. The running scene occupies the middle and lower portion with a beautiful asymmetrical rhythm.
SHORT EDITORIAL LINE: "Cuentos y mitos sobre la mujer salvaje". Place it in the bottom-right quiet lavender patch in smaller cream sans-serif across three concise lines, at a size that stays legible on mobile. Avoid stars scattered as filler, jewelry details, occult symbols, or extra animals.

Exact title: "Sobre la libertad"
Exact editorial line: "Los límites del poder sobre el individuo"
Art direction and content grounding: John Stuart Mill examines individuality, expression, and the limits of social power. Vivid cobalt field. A large cream profile with an expressive open mouth at right speaks a broad coral ribbon that crosses the center; the ribbon carries the title in very bold dark navy sans-serif, 'Sobre la' / 'libertad'. Three smaller quiet charcoal profiles in the lower-left listen, each different, no identical crowd, and no speech-bubble clutter. Elegant flat cut-paper drawing, powerful asymmetry, a clear voice making space for individuality. Editorial line in cream upper-left. No flags, fists or political logos.

Other matches distinct lengths and imperfect hand-cut edges. Monumental playful navy sans-serif 'Originales' across top, clean exact spelling. Small editorial line tucked at lower right. Sparse clever design with a sense of wit, no lightbulb or repetitive rainbow collage.

Use case: stylized-concept. One original finished Spanish book cover for Curva, 1024x1536 portrait. Input images 1 and 2 are STYLE REFERENCES ONLY: use their confident flat editorial drawings, huge carefully composed typography, charming simplified shapes, strong colors and left-edge fold. Invent a completely different composition from the references. This is a drawing, never a photograph or a realistic painting. Large clear silhouettes, clean organic contours, a few deliberately chosen details, elegant visual wit, high contrast and vibrant harmonious colors. No photorealism, no detailed anatomical rendering, no grain or paper/collage texture, no 3D plastic. At most one simple flat shadow shade per object. Objects have believable colors and people natural warm skin tones. Small expressive faces must have correct eyes and hands must be anatomically coherent and simplified. The collection must look varied: obey the individual typography, framing and palette below, not a repeated template. The illustration and title must be beautifully readable on a 190px-wide mobile card. Front-on cover fills the canvas with rounded corners, no perspective book mockup, no external shadow. MANDATORY FINISH: a narrow visible vertical pressed book-cover fold about 2% inward from the LEFT edge for the entire height, a fine highlight followed immediately by a subtle darker groove, matching the references. Keep the title clear of the fold and page edges. Print only the exact title and editorial line below with perfect Spanish accents. No author, year, brand, watermark or extra text.
Exact title: Cosmos
Exact editorial line: Una mirada al universo y a nuestro lugar en él
Art direction: Bright electric-cobalt sky. Huge title 'Cosmos' in warm cream exuberant heavy serif across the upper third, letters beautifully spaced. A monumental golden-orange ringed planet tilts diagonally through the center; its rings are broad flat coral and cream bands. A small deep-green-and-blue Earth floats in the bottom left; between planets a cream winding comet trajectory and three carefully placed simple star shapes create a sense of discovery. A coral red lower-right block of open sky carries the cream editorial line. Wonder-filled flat celestial travel poster, not realistic space rendering, no astronaut, no busy field of speckles, no extra planet labels.

In the lower half a small red-haired girl in a bright coral dress, drawn with dot eyes and simple natural light skin, kneels planting a seedling with a small blue trowel; behind her two other small graphically drawn children, one wearing an indigo cap and one in a mustard cardigan, share the garden quietly, no big faces. One friendly orange-breasted robin on the gate, large coral and golden flowers frame the bottom corners. Beautiful interlocking leaf shapes, botanical detail in a few deliberate lines, varied scale. The emerald upper foliage creates clean space for a warm-cream literary title; subtitle dark green on an open sun-yellow patch at lower left, clearly separated from faces and flowers. Palette feels like a vibrant garden in spring, not a rainbow or a muted watercolor. Illustration fills roughly two thirds, inviting secret place with a simple understandable story.
